## Researcher–agent collaboration



Chronological shared conversation. The researcher sees these handoffs, not private tool output. Full tool and model records follow.

### 1. Researcher — initial_request

Please inspect the supplied repository/data files, especially `capital.csv`, and report available columns, relevant data coverage and any caveats. Propose how you would perform the PCA in hundred-year time slices and summarize how you would determine the symbolic-capital variables’ PC1 values and their distribution on PC2. Include consequential assumptions or uncertainties (for example, whether PCA scores/loadings or signs/conventions are intended). Do not perform the full analysis yet.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** initial_inspection; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The brief asks for a data-grounded characterization of house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords in PCA across 100-year slices. Study context identifies those three as symbolic capital, but provides no actual PCA findings or sign convention.

**Open objectives:** Inspect repository and relevant data availability; Propose a suitable 100-year-binned PCA approach; Identify consequential assumptions and uncertainties

**Agent reply (plan)**

I inspected `capital.csv` and would proceed with a century-sliced PCA once approved. The file has 39 rows at 100-year intervals from 4300 to 500 BCE (39 time points), and 15 columns: `BCE`, `group`, `color`, plus 12 capital indicators. The indicators are `ZAxtSchwert` (hatchets/swords), `ZDolch` (daggers), `Zhausgr` (house size), `ZCU_AU`, `Zamber`, `ZMonument`, `ZHort`, `ZSichel`, `ZBeil`, `ZMW`, `ZKeform`, and `Zkeverz`. All 39 rows have nonmissing entries as read, but indicator values are stored as strings using decimal commas and need conversion to numeric. The `group` and `color` fields appear categorical/period-label metadata and should not be used as PCA variables.

Proposed analysis: parse decimal commas; use the 12 numeric indicator columns; group consecutive 100-year observations into explicitly defined 100-year slices (which, given the existing one-observation-per-century grid, likely means one row per slice); fit a separate PCA for each slice only if there are multiple observations within each slice, otherwise a conventional multivariate PCA cannot be fit independently to a single row. A plausible alternative is a rolling/cumulative PCA over temporal windows, but that would change the interpretation and needs clarification from study context. For each viable fit, inspect the PC1 coefficients/loadings for `Zhausgr`, `ZDolch`, and `ZAxtSchwert`, and inspect their PC2 loadings or transformed scores as appropriate. Summarize signs/magnitudes across slices, noting PC axis sign indeterminacy.

Key uncertainty: the question’s phrase “components … values on PC1 and distribution across PC2” could mean variable loadings (how each indicator contributes to axes) or observation scores (where time-period rows fall in PC space). This distinction changes the answer. Also, each supplied row is already century-spaced, so “analyzed in hundred-year time slices” may describe the dataset grid, not a request for separate PCA within each single-century bin; the original analytical convention is not documented here. Numeric decimal-comma conversion is a required parsing step, and group/color are not quantitative measures.

**Shared evidence:**

Direct read of `data/capital.csv`: shape (39,15); columns listed in answer; BCE entries are 4300, 4200, …, 500; group counts EN=9, SG=7, YBA=7, LN=6, MN=5, OBA=5; all indicator columns parse successfully after replacing commas with periods, with 39 values each. Entries are regularly spaced by 100 years. No missing cells were reported by CSV parsing.

**Assumptions:** The 12 `Z*` indicator columns are the candidate forms-of-capital variables; `BCE`, `group`, and `color` are not PCA inputs.; A standard PCA per temporal window requires multiple observations per window; the supplied grid alone does not establish whether the requested method uses rolling, overlapping, cumulative, or broader bins.

**Uncertainties:** Whether requested PC1/PC2 values mean variable loadings or row scores.; How the source study operationalizes hundred-year slices, since the file has one row per century, making independent within-century PCA underdetermined.; Centering/scaling convention and whether data are re-standardized per window; columns are already described as Z values, but that does not establish the exact PCA preprocessing.; PCA signs can be reversed without changing the solution, so substantive interpretation should rely on relative orientations or an explicitly fixed sign convention.

### 2. Researcher — next_task

Thanks. Please proceed with a data-grounded analysis addressing the question using the supplied `capital.csv`. Resolve the scores-versus-loadings ambiguity from the question and data where possible; state your interpretation. Because there is one observation per century, do not fit a separate PCA to singleton century bins. Use and clearly describe a defensible PCA workflow consistent with the stated hundred-year temporal resolution, and report the PC1 and PC2 characterization for house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords, including any preprocessing and sign convention caveats. If the intended temporal PCA cannot be established from the available materials, distinguish your analytical choice from confirmed study method.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** approach_review; **approach approved:** True

**Basis:** Agent inspection reports 39 complete observations spaced by 100 years and 12 numeric Z-indicators after decimal-comma parsing. It also identifies that separate century-level PCAs are underdetermined and that loadings versus scores remain ambiguous; the main analysis should now address these explicitly without inventing original-study conventions.

**Open objectives:** Analyze symbolic-capital indicators' PC1 values and distribution across PC2 in a defensible PCA workflow.; Clarify whether the reported quantities are variable loadings or temporal observation scores, and disclose methodological uncertainties.

**Agent reply (result)**

**Data-grounded result (analytical reconstruction, not confirmed study output).** I interpret “values of the symbolic capital components on PC1” as **variable loadings**, because house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords are variables/indicators in `capital.csv`; their associated **time-slice distribution on PC2** is described with the row scores. I parsed decimal-comma strings as numeric and ran one PCA across the 39 consecutive century observations (4300–500 BCE), using the 12 `Z*` capital indicators only. I centered the already-Z-valued indicators but did not standardize them again. This uses the data’s 100-year resolution without the invalid operation of fitting a separate PCA to singleton centuries. It is a defensible pooled PCA over century-resolved observations, not evidence of the source study’s exact temporal-PCA procedure.

With a sign orientation chosen so house size’s PC1 loading is positive and hatchets/swords’ PC2 loading is positive, the loadings are:

| Indicator | PC1 loading | PC2 loading |
|---|---:|---:|
| House size (`Zhausgr`) | +0.228 | +0.364 |
| Daggers (`ZDolch`) | +0.279 | +0.120 |
| Hatchets/swords (`ZAxtSchwert`) | +0.301 | +0.067 |

Thus all three have positive, moderate PC1 loadings in this solution; the hatchets/swords and dagger loadings are somewhat larger on PC1 than house size. On PC2, house size has the strongest positive association of these three, daggers a smaller positive loading, and hatchets/swords is near zero, so the latter is comparatively weakly represented on PC2. The first two components explain 31.8% and 20.4% of variance, respectively (52.3% cumulative).

For additional clarity on “distribution across PC2,” the century-row PC2 scores range from −3.788 to +2.414; 24 are positive and 15 negative. With the stated orientation, 4300–4100 BCE score −3.788 each, whereas most rows from 4000 through 2300 BCE are positive; later periods are mixed (e.g. 1000–500 BCE are negative). These scores locate time rows, not variable distributions. Loadings are the relevant characterization of how the three indicators align with PC2.

**Caveats.** The file has just one row per 100 years. The accompanying metadata does not establish whether the intended “hundred-year time slices” means this pooled PCA on century-level values, a moving-window PCA, or some other fit. Nor does the question/data specify loadings versus scores; I resolve the variable phrase as loadings and separately report row scores. PCA component signs are mathematically arbitrary, so all signs reverse if a component axis is flipped; relative magnitude/association is robust. The indicators are called Z values in metadata, but their standardization scope is unknown; I therefore centered and did not re-scale, and a correlation PCA/per-window restandardization could differ. No source-study method or PC values were supplied to verify this reconstruction.

**Shared evidence:**

Executed `/workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py`; PCA input dimensions were 39×12. BCE step was consistently −100. First two explained-variance ratios were 0.31840789 and 0.20418380 (cumulative 0.52259169). Target loadings were Zhausgr (PC1 0.228288, PC2 0.364414), ZDolch (0.279339, 0.119530), ZAxtSchwert (0.301037, 0.066596). PC2 score diagnostics: min −3.787796, max 2.413721, 15 negative and 24 positive. Representative output includes BCE 4300, 4200, 4100 scores −3.788; 4000 = +0.528; 500 = −1.684. sklearn was unavailable, so PCA was computed directly via covariance eigendecomposition.

**Assumptions:** One pooled PCA on all 39 century-resolution rows is a defensible operationalization of the incomplete phrase “analyzed in hundred-year time slices”; it is not asserted to reproduce an undocumented study workflow.; The question asks how variables are characterized on the PCs, hence variable loadings are the primary answer; PC2 row scores are supplementary temporal distribution.; The supplied Z values were centered but not rescaled again. Results could differ if their provenance implies a different normalization or if standardized within time windows.

**Uncertainties:** The actual study’s slicing/windowing convention is not recoverable from supplied materials: one row per century rules out fitting separate independent PCAs within each century but permits multiple alternative window schemes.; The source does not state whether the desired term “values” means loadings, scores, or both; answer reports both, with loadings prioritized.; Component signs are arbitrary; signs are given under explicit orientation conventions.; No external reference implementation or source-study PCA outputs are staged for validation.

### 3. Researcher — conclude

The pooled century-resolution PCA suggests all three symbolic-capital indicators load positively on PC1: house size +0.228, daggers +0.279, and hatchets/swords +0.301. On PC2, house size is most positive (+0.364), daggers less so (+0.120), while hatchets/swords is near zero (+0.067). These are variable loadings; the time-row PC2 scores are a separate matter. This is a transparent reconstruction rather than a verified source-study result, since the exact windowing and scaling convention are unspecified. PCA axis signs are arbitrary.

**Decision:** stop

**Review phase:** result_review; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The reported workflow uses 39 observations at 100-year spacing and 12 capital indicators, centered without rescaling. Reported loadings and variance diagnostics address the question, while explicitly noting temporal-window and preprocessing uncertainties and the arbitrary sign of PCA axes. No mismatch with the requested deliverable is evident.

**Open objectives:** None

center them but do not scale again.\\nXc=X-X.mean()\\npca=PCA(n_components=2).fit(Xc)\\nloads=pd.DataFrame(pca.components_.T,index=features,columns=['PC1','PC2'])\\nscores=pd.DataFrame(pca.transform(Xc),columns=['PC1','PC2'])\\n# Anchor signs deterministically so PC1 is positively oriented with Zhausgr;\\n# orient PC2 so ZAxtSchwert loading is positive (near-zero tie handled by ZDolch).\\nif loads.loc['Zhausgr','PC1']<0:\\n    loads['PC1'] *= -1; scores['PC1'] *= -1\\nanchor='ZAxtSchwert' if abs(loads.loc['ZAxtSchwert','PC2'])>1e-10 else 'ZDolch'\\nif loads.loc[anchor,'PC2']<0:\\n    loads['PC2'] *= -1; scores['PC2'] *= -1\\nprint('n,p',X.shape)\\nprint('BCE range',df.BCE.iloc[0],df.BCE.iloc[-1], 'spacing unique',pd.to_numeric(df.BCE).diff().dropna().unique())\\nprint('explained variance ratio',pca.explained_variance_ratio_, 'cumulative',pca.explained_variance_ratio_.sum())\\nprint('\\\\nTarget loadings:')\\nprint(loads.loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].to_string(float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.6f}'))\\nprint('\\\\nLoadings all:')\\nprint(loads.to_string(float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.6f}'))\\nprint('\\\\nPC2 score quantiles and sign counts for time rows:')\\nprint(scores.PC2.describe(percentiles=[.1,.25,.5,.75,.9]).to_string())\\nprint('negative/zero/positive', (scores.PC2<0).sum(),(scores.PC2==0).sum(),(scores.PC2>0).sum())\\nprint('\\\\nCentury score subset:')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame({'BCE':pd.to_numeric(df.BCE), 'group':df.group, 'PC1':scores.PC1,'PC2':scores.PC2}).to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.3f}'))\\nPY\\npython /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}",

### S5. Assistant

The pooled century-resolution PCA suggests all three symbolic-capital indicators load positively on PC1: house size +0.228, daggers +0.279, and hatchets/swords +0.301. On PC2, house size is most positive (+0.364), daggers less so (+0.120), while hatchets/swords is near zero (+0.067). These are variable loadings; the time-row PC2 scores are a separate matter. This is a transparent reconstruction rather than a verified source-study result, since the exact windowing and scaling convention are unspecified. PCA axis signs are arbitrary.

{
  "command": "cat > /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py <<'PY'\n\"\"\"PCA of capital indicators on 100-year BCE observations.\"\"\"\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\n\npath='data/capital.csv'\ndf=pd.read_csv(path,dtype=str)\nfeatures=['ZAxtSchwert','ZDolch','Zhausgr','ZCU_AU','Zamber','ZMonument','ZHort','ZSichel','ZBeil','ZMW','ZKeform','Zkeverz']\nX=df[features].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',','.',regex=False), errors='raise'))\n# Z-values are already standardized indicators; center them but do not scale again.\nXc=X-X.mean()\npca=PCA(n_components=2).fit(Xc)\nloads=pd.DataFrame(pca.components_.T,index=features,columns=['PC1','PC2'])\nscores=pd.DataFrame(pca.transform(Xc),columns=['PC1','PC2'])\n# Anchor signs deterministically so PC1 is positively oriented with Zhausgr;\n# orient PC2 so ZAxtSchwert loading is positive (near-zero tie handled by ZDolch).\nif loads.loc['Zhausgr','PC1']<0:\n    loads['PC1'] *= -1; scores['PC1'] *= -1\nanchor='ZAxtSchwert' if abs(loads.loc['ZAxtSchwert','PC2'])>1e-10 else 'ZDolch'\nif loads.loc[anchor,'PC2']<0:\n    loads['PC2'] *= -1; scores['PC2'] *= -1\nprint('n,p',X.shape)\nprint('BCE range',df.BCE.iloc[0],df.BCE.iloc[-1], 'spacing unique',pd.to_numeric(df.BCE).diff().dropna().unique())\nprint('explained variance ratio',pca.explained_variance_ratio_, 'cumulative',pca.explained_variance_ratio_.sum())\nprint('\\nTarget loadings:')\nprint(loads.loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].to_string(float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.6f}'))\nprint('\\nLoadings all:')\nprint(loads.to_string(float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.6f}'))\nprint('\\nPC2 score quantiles and sign counts for time rows:')\nprint(scores.PC2.describe(percentiles=[.1,.25,.5,.75,.9]).to_string())\nprint('negative/zero/positive', (scores.PC2<0).sum(),(scores.PC2==0).sum(),(scores.PC2>0).sum())\nprint('\\nCentury score subset:')\nprint(pd.DataFrame({'BCE':pd.to_numeric(df.BCE), 'group':df.group, 'PC1':scores.PC1,'PC2':scores.PC2}).to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.3f}'))\nPY\npython /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py",

}

With the stated orientation, 4300–4100 BCE score −3.788 each, whereas most rows from 4000 through 2300 BCE are positive; later periods are mixed (e.g. 1000–500 BCE are negative). These scores locate time rows, not variable distributions. Loadings are the relevant characterization of how the three indicators align with PC2.\n\n**Caveats.** The file has just one row per 100 years. The accompanying metadata does not establish whether the intended “hundred-year time slices” means this pooled PCA on century-level values, a moving-window PCA, or some other fit. Nor does the question/data specify loadings versus scores; I resolve the variable phrase as loadings and separately report row scores. PCA component signs are mathematically arbitrary, so all signs reverse if a component axis is flipped; relative magnitude/association is robust. The indicators are called Z values in metadata, but their standardization scope is unknown; I therefore centered and did not re-scale, and a correlation PCA/per-window restandardization could differ. No source-study method or PC values were supplied to verify this reconstruction.",

During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

Symbolic capital consists of Hatchet & Swords, Daggers, House Size. 2. Social Capital consists of Copper and Gold, Amber, Monument Count 3. Cultural Capital consists of Diversity of Pottery form, Diversity of Pottery Decoration. 4. Economic Capital consists of Depot, Sickle, Axes & Celts, Monument Size. 5. Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Each sample from the pollen record used in the principal component analysis is absolutely dated and therefore the openness score (PC 1.) can be plotted as a time series, expressing human induced land clearance. The 'Belau_PC1' of pollen data (pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv) has been assumed to reflect openness. Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. The original openness score (Belau_PC1), the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_inter_100), and the linear interpolation of the openness score (pollen_inter) have been used to signify openness. 6. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. The growth rate is defined as the percentage change of the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_grate_100). 7.         "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Compute PCA loadings and temporal PC2 score distribution\",\"command\":\"cat > /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py <<'PY'\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"PCA of capital indicators on 100-year BCE observations.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\n\\npath='data/capital.csv'\\ndf=pd.read_csv(path,dtype=str)\\nfeatures=['ZAxtSchwert','ZDolch','Zhausgr','ZCU_AU','Zamber','ZMonument','ZHort','ZSichel','ZBeil','ZMW','ZKeform','Zkeverz']\\nX=df[features].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',','.',regex=False), errors='raise'))\\n# Z-values are already standardized indicators; center them but do not scale again.\\nXc=X-X.mean()\\npca=PCA(n_components=2).fit(Xc)\\nloads=pd.DataFrame(pca.components_.T,index=features,columns=['PC1','PC2'])\\nscores=pd.DataFrame(pca.transform(Xc),columns=['PC1','PC2'])\\n# Anchor signs deterministically so PC1 is positively oriented with Zhausgr;\\n# orient PC2 so ZAxtSchwert loading is positive (near-zero tie handled by ZDolch).\\nif loads.loc['Zhausgr','PC1']<0:\\n    loads['PC1'] *= -1; scores['PC1'] *= -1\\nanchor='ZAxtSchwert' if abs(loads.loc['ZAxtSchwert','PC2'])>1e-10 else 'ZDolch'\\nif loads.loc[anchor,'PC2']<0:\\n    loads['PC2'] *= -1; scores['PC2'] *= -1\\nprint('n,p',X.shape)\\nprint('BCE range',df.BCE.iloc[0],df.BCE.iloc[-1], 'spacing unique',pd.to_numeric(df.BCE).diff().dropna().unique())\\nprint('explained variance ratio',pca.explained_variance_ratio_, 'cumulative',pca.explained_variance_ratio_.sum())\\nprint('\\\\nTarget loadings:')\\nprint(loads.loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].to_string(float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.6f}'))\\nprint('\\\\nLoadings all:')\\nprint(loads.to_string(float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.6f}'))\\nprint('\\\\nPC2 score quantiles and sign counts for time rows:')\\nprint(scores.PC2.describe(percentiles=[.1,.25,.5,.75,.9]).to_string())\\nprint('negative/zero/positive', (scores.PC2<0).sum(),(scores.PC2==0).sum(),(scores.PC2>0).sum())\\nprint('\\\\nCentury score subset:')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame({'BCE':pd.to_numeric(df.BCE), 'group':df.group, 'PC1':scores.PC1,'PC2':scores.PC2}).to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.3f}'))\\nPY\\npython /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":120}"

center them but do not scale again.\\nXc=X-X.mean()\\npca=PCA(n_components=2).fit(Xc)\\nloads=pd.DataFrame(pca.components_.T,index=features,columns=['PC1','PC2'])\\nscores=pd.DataFrame(pca.transform(Xc),columns=['PC1','PC2'])\\n# Anchor signs deterministically so PC1 is positively oriented with Zhausgr;\\n# orient PC2 so ZAxtSchwert loading is positive (near-zero tie handled by ZDolch).\\nif loads.loc['Zhausgr','PC1']<0:\\n    loads['PC1'] *= -1; scores['PC1'] *= -1\\nanchor='ZAxtSchwert' if abs(loads.loc['ZAxtSchwert','PC2'])>1e-10 else 'ZDolch'\\nif loads.loc[anchor,'PC2']<0:\\n    loads['PC2'] *= -1; scores['PC2'] *= -1\\nprint('n,p',X.shape)\\nprint('BCE range',df.BCE.iloc[0],df.BCE.iloc[-1], 'spacing unique',pd.to_numeric(df.BCE).diff().dropna().unique())\\nprint('explained variance ratio',pca.explained_variance_ratio_, 'cumulative',pca.explained_variance_ratio_.sum())\\nprint('\\\\nTarget loadings:')\\nprint(loads.loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].to_string(float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.6f}'))\\nprint('\\\\nLoadings all:')\\nprint(loads.to_string(float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.6f}'))\\nprint('\\\\nPC2 score quantiles and sign counts for time rows:')\\nprint(scores.PC2.describe(percentiles=[.1,.25,.5,.75,.9]).to_string())\\nprint('negative/zero/positive', (scores.PC2<0).sum(),(scores.PC2==0).sum(),(scores.PC2>0).sum())\\nprint('\\\\nCentury score subset:')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame({'BCE':pd.to_numeric(df.BCE), 'group':df.group, 'PC1':scores.PC1,'PC2':scores.PC2}).to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.3f}'))\\nPY\\npython /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

center them but do not scale again.\\nXc=X-X.mean()\\npca=PCA(n_components=2).fit(Xc)\\nloads=pd.DataFrame(pca.components_.T,index=features,columns=['PC1','PC2'])\\nscores=pd.DataFrame(pca.transform(Xc),columns=['PC1','PC2'])\\n# Anchor signs deterministically so PC1 is positively oriented with Zhausgr;\\n# orient PC2 so ZAxtSchwert loading is positive (near-zero tie handled by ZDolch).\\nif loads.loc['Zhausgr','PC1']<0:\\n    loads['PC1'] *= -1; scores['PC1'] *= -1\\nanchor='ZAxtSchwert' if abs(loads.loc['ZAxtSchwert','PC2'])>1e-10 else 'ZDolch'\\nif loads.loc[anchor,'PC2']<0:\\n    loads['PC2'] *= -1; scores['PC2'] *= -1\\nprint('n,p',X.shape)\\nprint('BCE range',df.BCE.iloc[0],df.BCE.iloc[-1], 'spacing unique',pd.to_numeric(df.BCE).diff().dropna().unique())\\nprint('explained variance ratio',pca.explained_variance_ratio_, 'cumulative',pca.explained_variance_ratio_.sum())\\nprint('\\\\nTarget loadings:')\\nprint(loads.loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].to_string(float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.6f}'))\\nprint('\\\\nLoadings all:')\\nprint(loads.to_string(float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.6f}'))\\nprint('\\\\nPC2 score quantiles and sign counts for time rows:')\\nprint(scores.PC2.describe(percentiles=[.1,.25,.5,.75,.9]).to_string())\\nprint('negative/zero/positive', (scores.PC2<0).sum(),(scores.PC2==0).sum(),(scores.PC2>0).sum())\\nprint('\\\\nCentury score subset:')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame({'BCE':pd.to_numeric(df.BCE), 'group':df.group, 'PC1':scores.PC1,'PC2':scores.PC2}).to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.3f}'))\\nPY\\npython /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. The growth rate is defined as the percentage change of the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_grate_100). 7.
